You: ## PROJECT CONTEXT
You are working on `ollama-repl`, a Claude-Code-style terminal agent REPL written in JavaScript for the QuickJS (`qjsm`) runtime. 
- Runtime: QuickJS only. No Node.js. No `require`, no `node:path`, no `node:fs`. Use `std`, `os`, and `lib/paths.js` helpers.
- Main file: `repl.js` (~2700 lines). Holds the main closure, system prompts, and command routing.
- Key path files: `lib/paths.js` (expandHome, unquote), `lib/file-refs.js` (attachFiles, formatFileBlocks), `lib/file-blocks.js` (isSafeRelativePath), `lib/tool-requests.js` (executeTool).
- The project root/current working directory is held in `opts.root` (changeable via `/cd`).

## THE TASK
Update the harness so the model understands and uses homedir (`~`), relative (`./`), and absolute paths correctly. This requires changes to the system prompt, outbound path normalization (harness -> model), and inbound path resolution (model -> harness).

### 1. Update the System Prompt
In `repl.js`, update `BUILTIN_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (and ensure it flows through to `SYSTEM_PROMPT`). Add a clear "Path Conventions" section. 

Add this text to the prompt:
Path Conventions:
~ refers to your home directory (e.g., ~/config.json).
./ refers to the project root (current working directory). The project root is currently: [INSERT_ROOT_HERE].
Absolute paths (e.g., /home/user/project/src/foo.js) are allowed but will be shown to you as relative paths (e.g., ./src/foo.js) if they fall within the project root.
When calling tools (read_file, edit_file, etc.), prefer ./ relative paths. You may use ~ for homedir files.
Never use .. to escape the project root.
*Implementation detail:* Since `opts.root` can change via `/cd`, you should either inject the current root dynamically when the prompt is built, or just state the rule and let the tool results show the relative paths. If injecting dynamically, update `messages[0].content` when `/cd` is run (similar to how `systemNotes(opts.root)` is already appended).

### 2. Outbound Path Normalization (Harness -> Model)
Before sending text to the model (in prompts, attached files, or tool results), absolute paths that reside *inside* `opts.root` must be rewritten as `./relative/path`.

Places to update:
- `lib/file-refs.js`: In `formatFileBlocks()` and `formatFileRefs()`, rewrite the `path` property of attached files to be relative to `opts.root` (prefixed with `./`) if they are inside it.
- `repl.js`: In `gatherProjectContext()`, rewrite the layout listing paths to use `./`.
- `lib/tool-requests.js`: In the output of `read_file`, `list_directory`, and `grep`, rewrite any absolute file paths in the output text to be relative to `opts.root` (prefixed with `./`).
- `repl.js`: In `formatFileBlocks` or wherever `File: path` headers are generated for the model's own writes, ensure they are relative.

*Helper logic:* Create or use a helper function `toRelativePath(absPath, root)`:
```javascript
// Returns './relative/path' if absPath is inside root, else returns absPath unchanged.
function toRelativePath(absPath, root) {
  const normRoot = root.endsWith('/') ? root : root + '/';
  if (absPath.startsWith(normRoot)) {
    return './' + absPath.slice(normRoot.length);
  }
  if (absPath === root) return './';
  return absPath;
}
3. Inbound Path Resolution (Model -> Harness)
When the model calls a tool with a path, the harness must correctly resolve it before checking safety or reading the file.
lib/paths.js: Ensure expandHome(path) is called on all inbound tool arguments. It already handles ~ and ~user.
lib/file-blocks.js / lib/tool-requests.js: Update isSafeRelativePath (or the path validation logic in executeTool) to:
Accept paths starting with ./.
Accept paths starting with ~ (after expanding them).
Accept absolute paths, BUT resolve them and verify they fall within opts.root. If an absolute path is inside opts.root, normalize it to a relative path internally before processing.
Reject paths containing .. that would escape opts.root.
4. Constraints & Rules
Do NOT use Node's path module. Use string manipulation or existing lib/paths.js functions.
opts.root is the source of truth for the "current directory". It is an absolute path (resolved via realpath in resolveDir).
When rewriting paths in tool results, be careful not to break JSON formatting or code blocks. Only rewrite paths that are clearly file paths (e.g., in read_file headers, list_directory outputs, or File: headers).
Run cmake . and cmake --install . in the repo root after editing files under lib/.
Run smoke.sh to verify the harness still loads and basic commands work.
5. Verification Steps
Start a session. Check the system prompt (/system) to ensure the Path Conventions section is present and mentions the current root.
Ask the model to list_directory the root. Verify the output shows ./filename instead of /absolute/filename.
Ask the model to read_file a file using an absolute path (e.g., /home/roman/Sources/quickjs/qjs-lws/examples/ollama-repl/repl.js). Verify it succeeds and the tool result shows the content with relative path headers.
Ask the model to read_file ~/some_file.txt. Verify it resolves correctly.
Check that edit_file with a ./ path works.
Check that edit_file with ../ (escaping root) is rejected.

File: lib/paths.js
```javascript
/**
 * paths: shell-style home expansion for paths the user types (/cd, Tab
 * completion).
 *
 *   expandHome('~/Sources')        -> '/home/roman/Sources'
 *   expandHome('~rsenn/x')         -> '/home/rsenn/x'   (from /etc/passwd)
 *   expandHome('src')              -> 'src'
 *   unquote('"My Dir"'), unquote('My\\ Dir')  -> 'My Dir'
 */
import { getenv, loadFile } from 'std';

/** home directory of `user` from /etc/passwd, undefined if unknown */
export function homeOf(user) {
  for(const line of (loadFile('/etc/passwd') ?? '').split('\n')) {
    const f = line.split(':');
    if(f[0] === user && f[5]) return f[5];
  }
}

/** path with a leading ~ or ~user replaced by that home directory, else unchanged */
export function expandHome(path) {
  const m = /^~([^/]*)(\/.*)?$/.exec(path);
  if(!m) return path;

  const home = m[1] ? homeOf(m[1]) : (getenv('HOME') ?? homeOf(getenv('USER') ?? ''));
  return home ? home.replace(/\/$/, '') + (m[2] ?? '') : path;
}

/** surrounding quotes and backslash escapes removed */
export function unquote(s) {
  s = s.trim();
  if(s.length > 1 && (s[0] === '"' || s[0] === "'") && s[s.length - 1] === s[0]) return s.slice(1, -1);
  return s.replace(/\\(.)/g, '$1');
}

```

File: lib/file-refs.js
```javascript
/**
 * Detects file paths and glob patterns typed into a chat prompt (e.g. "fix
 * the bug in src/foo.js" or "review *.md") and reads their contents so they
 * can be attached to the outgoing message - the same "@file" convenience
 * Claude Code's own prompt does, just token-scanned instead of an explicit
 * `@` syntax.
 */
import { stat, S_IFREG } from 'os';
import { loadFile } from 'std';
import { globMatch, isGlobPattern, walk, fileMode } from './match.js';
import { referenceFiles } from './reference-files.js';
import { directDependencies } from './imports.js';

/* A path-shaped token: contains a `/`, or a glob metacharacter, or ends in
   a plausible file extension. Deliberately permissive - false positives
   are filtered out below by actually trying to resolve them on disk. */
const TOKEN_RE = /[./]*[\w.\-\/*?[\]{}]*[\w*?\]][\w.\-\/*?[\]{}]*/g;

/* what goes into the message as text: a file over ATTACH_MAX_BYTES, generated or binary, or one past the count or total budget
   is only named (see formatFileRefs()) and the model reads it with read_file when it needs it */
const MAX_FILES = 12;
export const ATTACH_MAX_BYTES = 10 * 1024;
const MAX_TOTAL_BYTES = 40 * 1024;
/* largest file read just to count its lines for the reference (and to spot a binary) */
const COUNT_MAX_BYTES = 512 * 1024;
const MAX_REFS = 20;
const BINARY_EXT = /\.(png|jpe?g|gif|webp|ico|bmp|pdf|zip|gz|xz|bz2|zst|tar|7z|so|o|a|dll|exe|bin|wasm|class|jar|mp[34]|mkv|mov|woff2?|ttf|otf|sqlite3?|db|cpio)$/i;
const GENERATED = /(^|\/)(node_modules|\.git|build[^/]*|dist|out)\/|\.min\.(js|css)$|(^|\/)(package-lock\.json|yarn\.lock|pnpm-lock\.yaml)$/;
const MAX_DIR_FILES = 4;
export const SOURCE_EXT = new Set(['js', 'mjs', 'cjs', 'jsx', 'ts', 'tsx', 'c', 'h', 'cpp', 'hpp', 'html', 'css', 'md']);

function looksLikePath(token) {
  if(token.length < 2) return false;
  if(isGlobPattern(token)) {
    // Require at least one alphanumeric character to avoid matching
    // pure glob metacharacters like "***", "**", "???" which commonly
    // appear in make output and would expand to every file.
    return /[A-Za-z0-9]/.test(token);
  }
  if(token.endsWith('/')) return true;
  if(token.includes('/')) return true;
  return /\.[A-Za-z0-9]{1,10}$/.test(token) && !/^\d+\.\d+$/.test(token); // skip bare "2.5"-style numbers
}

/** Up to `MAX_DIR_FILES` source files under `dir` (recursively) - a bare
    directory reference ending in "/" triggers this instead of a single-
    file lookup ("src/" -> a handful of its source files, not a literal
    file named "src/"). Ordered top-level-first (files directly in `dir`
    before anything in a subdirectory), then newest-first within each
    depth - representative of the directory's own top rather than
    whichever subdirectory happens to have the single newest file, and
    capped well below "every file in there" so a broad directory
    reference can't flood the request. */
function newestSourceFiles(dir, root) {
  const base = dir ? (root === '.' ? dir : `${root}/${dir}`) : root;
  const candidates = [];

  for(const path of walk(base, root)) {
    const rel = root === '.' ? path : path.slice(root.length + 1);
    const ext = rel.slice(rel.lastIndexOf('.') + 1).toLowerCase();

    if(!SOURCE_EXT.has(ext)) continue;

    const [st] = stat(root === '.' ? rel : `${root}/${rel}`);
    if(!st) continue;

    const withinDir = dir ? rel.slice(dir.length + 1) : rel;
    const depth = withinDir.split('/').length - 1; // 0 = directly in `dir`, 1 = one subdirectory down, ...

    candidates.push({ path: rel, mtime: st.mtime, depth });
  }

  candidates.sort((a, b) => a.depth - b.depth || b.mtime - a.mtime);

  return candidates.slice(0, MAX_DIR_FILES).map(c => c.path);
}

function stripPunctuation(token) {
  return token.replace(/^[,.;:()'"`]+/, '').replace(/[,.;:()'"`]+$/, '');
}

/**
 * Scans `text` for file/glob references, resolves them against `root`, and
 * returns `{ text, files }` - `files` is `[{ path, content, dependencies }]`
 * for every match that actually resolved to a readable regular file,
 * deduped, capped at `MAX_FILES`/`MAX_TOTAL_BYTES` so a runaway glob can't
 * flood the request. `dependencies` is that file's own direct imports/
 * includes (paths only, see `directDependencies()` - `lib/imports.js`) -
 * not attached, just named, so the model can `read_file` whichever it
 * actually needs instead of every attached file dragging its dependency chain along
 * unasked. `text` is unchanged; callers combine it with `files` themselves
 * (see buildMessage() in repl.js) so detection stays independent of
 * formatting.
 */
export function extractFileRefs(text, root = '.') {
  const candidates = new Set();

  /* "lib/path/<function-name>.c" / "lib/{name}.c" are templates, not paths:
     without this the directory part ("lib/path/") alone was picked up and
     four arbitrary files from it attached. */
  const scanned = text.replace(/\S*[<{][^>}\s]*[>}]\S*/g, ' ');

  for(const raw of scanned.match(TOKEN_RE) ?? []) {
    const token = stripPunctuation(raw);
    if(looksLikePath(token)) candidates.add(token);
  }

  /* label (shown to the model, and used as the write-back path if the
     model echoes it in a "File:" block) -> the actual path to read from -
     the same for project files, but different for referenceFiles() entries
     (label is just the bare name, e.g. "quickjs.h"; the actual path is
     wherever that's installed or checked out). */
  const paths = new Map();

  /* Labels eligible for directDependencies() below - every root-relative
     match (direct file, glob, directory pick), but not a referenceFiles()
     entry: those live outside `root` entirely (an installed/checked-out
     location), so resolving *their* imports/includes against `root`
     wouldn't mean anything. */
  const projectRelative = new Set();
  const referenceFilePaths = referenceFiles(root);

  for(const token of candidates) {
    if(token.endsWith('/')) {
      const dir = token.replace(/\/+$/, '');

      for(const p of newestSourceFiles(dir, root)) {
        paths.set(p, root === '.' ? p : `${root}/${p}`);
        projectRelative.add(p);
      }
      continue;
    }

    if(isGlobPattern(token)) {
      for(const p of globMatch(token, root)) {
        paths.set(p, root === '.' ? p : `${root}/${p}`);
        projectRelative.add(p);
      }
      continue;
    }

    const full = root === '.' ? token : `${root}/${token}`;

    if(fileMode(full) === S_IFREG) {
      paths.set(token, full);
      projectRelative.add(token);
    } else if(referenceFilePaths[token]) {
      paths.set(token, referenceFilePaths[token]);
    }
  }

  const files = [];
  let totalBytes = 0;
  const skipped = [];
  const large = [];

  for(const [label, full] of paths) {
    const [st] = stat(full);

    if(!st || BINARY_EXT.test(label)) {
      skipped.push(label);
      continue;
    }

    const why = GENERATED.test(label) ? 'generated' : st.size > ATTACH_MAX_BYTES ? 'too large' : files.length >= MAX_FILES || totalBytes + st.size > MAX_TOTAL_BYTES ? 'over the budget' : null;

    if(why) {
      /* named only: its size and lines tell the model whether to read it whole or by line range */
      const text = st.size <= COUNT_MAX_BYTES ? loadFile(full) : null;
      if(text?.includes('\0')) skipped.push(label);
      else large.push({ path: label, size: st.size, lines: text == null ? null : text.split('\n').length - (text.endsWith('\n') ? 1 : 0), why });
      continue;
    }

    const content = loadFile(full);
    if(content == null || content.includes('\0')) {
      skipped.push(label);
      continue;
    }

    const dependencies = projectRelative.has(label) ? directDependencies(label, content, root) : [];

    files.push({ path: label, content, dependencies });
    totalBytes += st.size;
  }

  return { files, skipped, large };
}

const EXT_LANG = {
  js: 'javascript',
  mjs: 'javascript',
  cjs: 'javascript',
  ts: 'typescript',
  jsx: 'jsx',
  tsx: 'tsx',
  json: 'json',
  py: 'python',
  c: 'c',
  h: 'c',
  cpp: 'cpp',
  hpp: 'cpp',
  sh: 'bash',
  md: 'markdown',
  html: 'html',
  css: 'css',
  yml: 'yaml',
  yaml: 'yaml',
  log: 'text',
  txt: 'cmake',
  cmake: 'cmake',
  ac: 'aclocal',
  in: 'autotools',
  am: 'automake',
  'clang-format': 'yaml',
  crt: 'pem/x509',
  key: 'pem/x509',
  pem: 'pem',
  'sublime-project': 'json/sublime-text',
  'sublime-workspace': 'json/sublime-text',
  prettierrc: 'json',
  git: 'text',
  gitignore: 'magic',
  'cmake-format': 'magic',
  gitmodules: 'text',
  diff: 'diff',
  patch: 'diff',
  sqlite: 'sqlite',
  sqlite3: 'sqlite',
  project: 'xml/eclipse',
  cproject: 'xml/eclipse',
};

export function languageFor(path) {
  const ext = path.slice(path.lastIndexOf('.') + 1).toLowerCase();
  return EXT_LANG[ext] ?? '';
}

/** Renders `files` as "File: path" + fenced-code blocks (plus a
    dependencies note when `extractFileRefs()` found any - see its own
    doc comment), the same shape the assistant is instructed (see
    SYSTEM_PROMPT in repl.js) to reply with. */
/** "53 KB", "812 B", "1.4 MB" */
const fmtSize = n => (n < 1024 ? `${n} B` : n < 1048576 ? `${Math.round(n / 102.4) / 10} KB` : `${Math.round(n / 104857.6) / 10} MB`);

/** the files that were not attached, by name in backticks with size and lines, and how to get at them */
export function formatFileRefs(large) {
  if(!large?.length) return '';

  const rows = large.slice(0, MAX_REFS).map(f => `- \`${f.path}\` (${f.lines != null ? `${f.lines} lines, ` : ''}${fmtSize(f.size)}${f.why === 'too large' ? '' : `, ${f.why}`})`);
  if(large.length > MAX_REFS) rows.push(`- ... and ${large.length - MAX_REFS} more`);

  return `Not attached (too large, generated or over the size budget of this message): read the part you need with read_file (path, and start/end for a line range), do not guess what is in them:\n${rows.join('\n')}`;
}

export function formatFileBlocks(files) {
  return files
    .map(f => {
      const deps = f.dependencies?.length ? `\n(imports/includes: ${f.dependencies.join(', ')} - read_file any of these you actually need)` : '';
      return `File: ${f.path}\n\`\`\`${languageFor(f.path)}\n${f.content}\n\`\`\`${deps}`;
    })
    .join('\n\n');
}

```

File: lib/file-blocks.js
```javascript
/**
 * Parses "File: path\n```lang\n...content...\n```" blocks out of an
 * assistant reply and writes them into the local project tree. This is the
 * one output convention the system prompt (repl.js) asks the model to
 * follow for any file it wants to create or modify - deliberately simple
 * and unambiguous to parse, rather than trying to guess intent from
 * arbitrary fenced code blocks.
 */
import { stat, mkdir } from 'os';
import { open as fopen } from 'std';

/* blank lines between the path and the fence, and a bare language line in
   front of it, are tolerated: chat sites render code blocks that way */
const FILE_BLOCK_RE = /^File:[ \t]*(\S+)[ \t]*\r?\n(?:[ \t]*\r?\n)*(?:[A-Za-z0-9_+-]+[ \t]*\r?\n)?```[^\n`]*\r?\n([\s\S]*?)\r?\n```[ \t]*$/gm;
const FENCE_RE = /```([a-zA-Z0-9_+-]*)[ \t]*\r?\n([\s\S]*?)\r?\n```/g;

/** Every `{ path, content }` file block found in `text`. */
export function extractFileBlocks(text) {
  const blocks = [];
  let m;

  FILE_BLOCK_RE.lastIndex = 0;

  while((m = FILE_BLOCK_RE.exec(text))) blocks.push({ path: m[1].replaceAll(/(^[`'"]|[`'"]$)/g, ''), content: m[2] });

  return blocks;
}

const LANG_EXT = {
  javascript: 'js',
  js: 'js',
  jsx: 'jsx',
  typescript: 'ts',
  ts: 'ts',
  tsx: 'tsx',
  json: 'json',
  python: 'py',
  py: 'py',
  c: 'c',
  h: 'c',
  cpp: 'cpp',
  'c++': 'cpp',
  hpp: 'cpp',
  html: 'html',
  css: 'css',
  bash: 'sh',
  sh: 'sh',
  shell: 'sh',
  markdown: 'md',
  md: 'md',
  yaml: 'yaml',
  yml: 'yaml',
};

/** File extension (no dot) for a fenced-code-block language tag, "txt" for anything unrecognized/empty. */
export function extensionFor(lang) {
  return LANG_EXT[(lang || '').toLowerCase()] ?? 'txt';
}

/**
 * Every fenced code block in `text` that ISN'T claimed by a "File:" label
 * (extractFileBlocks() above) - the "never miss a file in a response"
 * fallback for a reply that includes unlabeled code. Returned as
 * `{ lang, content }` in reply order; callers (saveAllBlocks() below)
 * assign the actual output path.
 */
export function extractAnonymousBlocks(text) {
  const namedRanges = [];
  let m;

  FILE_BLOCK_RE.lastIndex = 0;
  while((m = FILE_BLOCK_RE.exec(text))) namedRanges.push([m.index, m.index + m[0].length]);

  const blocks = [];

  FENCE_RE.lastIndex = 0;
  while((m = FENCE_RE.exec(text))) {
    if(namedRanges.some(([start, end]) => m.index >= start && m.index < end)) continue;
    blocks.push({ lang: m[1], content: m[2] });
  }

  return blocks;
}

function mkdirp(dir) {
  if(!dir || dir === '.' || dir === '/') return;

  const [st] = stat(dir);
  if(st) return;

  mkdirp(dir.slice(0, dir.lastIndexOf('/')));
  mkdir(dir);
}

/** Rejects paths that would escape `root` (`../..`, an absolute path, ...). */
function isSafeRelativePath(path) {
  if(path.startsWith('/') || path.startsWith('~')) return false;

  const parts = path.split('/');
  let depth = 0;

  for(const part of parts) {
    if(part === '..') {
      depth--;
      if(depth < 0) return false;
    } else if(part !== '.' && part !== '') {
      depth++;
    }
  }

  return true;
}

/**
 * Writes every block from `extractFileBlocks()` under `root`, creating
 * parent directories as needed. Returns `{ written, rejected }` - paths
 * that fail `isSafeRelativePath()` are reported in `rejected` and never
 * touch the filesystem.
 *
 * @param {import('./file-exchange.js').FileExchange} [fileExchange] - if
 *   given, each path's old-vs-new diff is recorded (see
 *   FileExchange#recordReceived()) *before* it's overwritten.
 */
export async function saveFileBlocks(blocks, root = '.', fileExchange) {
  const written = [];
  const rejected = [];

  for(const { path, content } of blocks) {
    if(!isSafeRelativePath(path)) {
      rejected.push(path);
      continue;
    }

    if(fileExchange) await fileExchange.recordReceived(path, content);

    const full = root === '.' ? path : `${root}/${path}`;
    const dir = full.includes('/') ? full.slice(0, full.lastIndexOf('/')) : null;

    if(dir) mkdirp(dir);

    const f = fopen(full, 'w');
    if(!f) {
      rejected.push(path);
      continue;
    }

    f.puts(content);
    f.close();
    written.push(path);
  }

  return { written, rejected };
}

/**
 * Saves *every* code block in a reply - "File:"-labeled ones under their
 * given path, and any remaining unlabeled fenced block (see
 * extractAnonymousBlocks() above) under an auto-assigned
 * "<model>-output-N.ext" path from `sentFiles` (lib/sent-files.js) - so a
 * reply never silently drops code the model produced just because it
 * didn't follow the "File:" convention for it. Every path actually
 * written (named or auto-named) is registered with `sentFiles.add()`.
 */
export async function saveAllBlocks(reply, { root = '.', sentFiles, fileExchange, anonymous: saveAnonymous = false } = {}) {
  const named = extractFileBlocks(reply);
  /* Unlabeled code blocks are only saved on request: now that the model
     edits through tools, they are mostly examples/answers, and saving them
     littered the project with <model>-output-N.txt files. */
  const anonymous = (saveAnonymous ? extractAnonymousBlocks(reply) : []).map(({ lang, content }) => ({
    path: sentFiles.nextOutputPath(extensionFor(lang)),
    content,
  }));

  const { written, rejected } = await saveFileBlocks([...named, ...anonymous], root, fileExchange);

  for(const path of written) sentFiles.add(path);

  return { written, rejected };
}

```
(imports/includes: lib/file-exchange.js - read_file any of these you actually need)

File: smoke.sh
```bash
#!/bin/sh
# smoke.sh [REPL.js]: checks that ollama-repl really starts and answers
#
# `qjsm repl.js --help` passes with a missing import or an out-of-scope
# variable, since those only fail when a command runs. This script
#
#   1. imports every lib/*.js on its own (truncated file, missing export),
#      and runs test-rawcolor.js, test-availability.js, test-delegates.js and test-skills.js
#   2. starts the REPL in a pty in an empty directory, sends a fixed set of
#      commands and fails on any error, exception or unhandled rejection
#
# SMOKE_ARGS adds options to the REPL's command line (the restart passes its own).
# Exit status: 0 all good, 1 a check failed (the output says which).
# Used by hand, and by the self-restart (prepareRestart in repl.js).

REPL=${1:-$(dirname "$0")/repl.js}
DIR=$(cd "$(dirname "$REPL")" && pwd)
REPL=$DIR/$(basename "$REPL")
WAIT=${SMOKE_WAIT:-4}
BAD='error:|Error:|nhandled|is not defined|is read-only|Cannot read'
FAILED=0

# 1. every module loads on its own
for f in "$DIR"/lib/*.js; do
  OUT=$(qjsm -e "import('$f').then(() => 0, e => import('std').then(s => { console.log(e.message); s.exit(1); }))" 2>&1) ||
    { echo "FAIL import $f: $OUT"; FAILED=1; }
done

# 1b. the colouring of the verbose view only adds colour
OUT=$(cd "$DIR" && qjsm test-rawcolor.js 2>&1) || { echo "FAIL rawcolor: $OUT"; FAILED=1; }

# 1d. the delegate memory ranks and learns
OUT=$(cd "$DIR" && qjsm test-delegates.js 2>&1) || { echo "FAIL delegates: $OUT"; FAILED=1; }

# 1e. skills are found in both directories

WORK=$(mktemp -d) || exit 1
SOCK=/tmp/or-sm-$$.s # short, to stay clear of the 107 byte limit

  sleep "$WAIT"

    printf '%s\r' "$c"
    sleep 1
  done
} | timeout 60 script -qec "qjsm '$REPL' --listen '$SOCK' $SMOKE_ARGS" /dev/null > "$LOG" 2>&1
STATUS=$?

  echo "FAIL repl printed errors:"
  head -n 8 "$WORK/bad.txt"
  FAILED=1

exit $FAILED

const SYSTEM_PROMPT = userSystemPrompt() ?? BUILTIN_SYSTEM_PROMPT;

/* Only appended for QuickJS projects (see main()) - it is noise, and costs
   context a small model needs, anywhere else. */
const QJS_NOTES = `

This project runs on QuickJS (qjs/qjsm), not Node.js - don't assume Node's
globals, module system ("require", "node_modules"), or built-ins ("path",
"crypto", "buffer", "process.nextTick") are available. Before writing code
that touches the filesystem, environment, process, or child processes,
check (read_file/run_command - e.g. grep for "from 'process'"/"from 'fs'"/
"from 'util'"/"from 'child_process'") whether the file/project you're
editing already imports one of qjs-modules' own built-ins (process/fs/
util/child_process) - if so, match that existing convention; otherwise
default to QuickJS's own std/os modules, since those are always present
with no extra dependency (quickjs.md, one read_file away, is the
reference). This only constrains code meant to run inside qjs/qjsm itself
- write ordinary browser-side JS when that's what's actually being asked

Writing a new native (C) QuickJS binding is a common request here - when
one of examples/fib.c, examples/point.c, and the quickjs-binding-api.md

Tool (read_file): /**
 * Native tool schemas (`TOOLS`, passed as `tools` to client.chat()/
 * chatStream() - see API.md) and their execution (`executeTool()`), for
 * the tool-calling loop in repl.js (`runToolLoop()`) - replaces the
 * earlier LIST:/READ:/RUN: plain-text-in-the-reply-body protocol with
 * real, provider-native function calling.
 */
import { stat, S_IFREG, S_IFDIR } from 'os';
import { rmSync } from 'fs';

/* rmSync throws on a missing file, os.remove did not */
const remove = path => rmSync(path, { force: true });
import { loadFile, open as fopen } from 'std';
import { walk, fileMode } from './match.js';
import { runCommand } from './run-command.js';
import { expandHome } from './paths.js';
import { aliasToolCall } from './text-tool-calls.js';
import { getenv } from 'std';
import { qjsProjectDirs } from './reference-files.js';

const LISTABLE_EXT = new Set(['js', 'mjs', 'cjs', 'jsx', 'ts', 'tsx', 'c', 'h', 'cpp', 'hpp', 'html', 'css', 'md', 'cmake', 'txt', 'sh']);
const MAX_READ_BYTES = 64 * 1024;
const MAX_GREP_LINES = 60;
const MAX_LIST_ENTRIES = 300;

function isListable(name) {
  const base = name.slice(name.lastIndexOf('/') + 1);
  if(/^readme/i.test(base)) return true;

  const ext = base.slice(base.lastIndexOf('.') + 1).toLowerCase();
  return LISTABLE_EXT.has(ext);
}

/** Every listable (JS/C/HTML/CSS/Markdown + README*) file under `dir` (relative to `root`), sorted. */
export function listFiles(dir, root) {
  const base = dir ? (root === '.' ? dir : `${root}/${dir}`) : root;
  const out = [];

  const filetime = (f, t = 'mtime') => stat(f)?.[0][t];

  for(const path of walk(base, root)) {
    const rel = root === '.' ? path : path.slice(root.length + 1);
    if(isListable(rel)) out.push([filetime(path), rel]);
    if(out.length >= MAX_LIST_ENTRIES) break;
  }

  return out.sort((a, b) => b[0] - a[0]).map(([tm, fn]) => fn);
}

/** Model-given path made usable: quotes, file:// and $HOME / ${HOME} / ~ expanded, "./" prefix dropped. */
function normalizePath(p) {
  p = String(p).trim().replace(/^(['"`])(.*)\1$/, '$2').replace(/^file:\/\//, '');
  p = p.replace(/^\$\{?HOME\}?(?=\/|$)/, getenv('HOME') ?? '$&');
  p = expandHome(p);
  while(p.startsWith('./')) p = p.slice(2);
  return p === '.' ? '' : p;
}

/**
 * If `arg` names (or starts with, path-fashion) a sibling "qjs-*" project
 * (reference-files.js's `qjsProjectDirs()`), resolves it against that
 * project's own directory instead of `root` - so `list_directory`/
 * `read_file` with "qjs-modules" or "qjs-modules/quickjs-archive.c" reach
 * into the sibling project the same way they already reach into this one.
 * Falls back to `root` unchanged for anything that isn't a known project
 * name.
 */
function resolveBase(arg, root) {
  arg = normalizePath(arg);

  /* absolute (after ~ expansion): used as is, not joined to root */
  if(arg.startsWith('/')) return { base: '.', rel: arg, prefix: '' };

  const slash = arg.indexOf('/');
  const name = slash === -1 ? arg : arg.slice(0, slash);
  const dir = qjsProjectDirs(root).get(name);

  if(dir == null) return { base: root, rel: arg, prefix: '' };

  return { base: dir, rel: slash === -1 ? '' : arg.slice(slash + 1), prefix: name };
}

/** Lines `start`..`end` (1-based, inclusive; both optional) of a file. */
function readFile(path, root, start, end) {
  const full = root === '.' || path.startsWith('/') ? path : `${root}/${path}`;

  const mode = fileMode(full);
  if(mode === 0) {
    console.warn(`warning: file not found: ${full} - check the path with list_directory`);
    return `(not found: ${full} - check the path with list_directory, or use an absolute path or ~/...)`;
  }
  if(mode !== S_IFREG) {
    console.warn(`warning: not a regular file: ${full} (${mode === S_IFDIR ? 'directory' : 'special file'})`);
    return `(${full} is not a regular file${mode === S_IFDIR ? ' but a directory - use list_directory' : ''})`;
  }

  let content = loadFile(full);
  if(content == null) return `(cannot read ${full}: permission denied or unreadable)`;

  if(start || end) {
    const lines = content.split('\n');
    const a = Math.max(1, +start || 1);
    const b = Math.min(lines.length, +end || lines.length);
    return `(lines ${a}-${b} of ${lines.length})\n${lines.slice(a - 1, b).join('\n')}`;
  }

  return content.length > MAX_READ_BYTES ? content.slice(0, MAX_READ_BYTES) + `\n... (truncated at ${MAX_READ_BYTES} bytes of ${content.length} - read the rest with start/end line numbers)` : content;
}

/** Path inside `root` that a tool may write: relative, no "..". Returns the full path or null. */
function writablePath(path, root) {
  if(typeof path !== 'string' || !path) {
    console.warn('warning: path argument is missing or not a string');
    return null;
  }
  if( path.startsWith('/') || path.split('/').includes('..')) {
    console.warn('warning: path starts with / or contains .. segment and is rejected');
    return null;
  }
  return root === '.' ? path : `${root}/${path}`;
}

const pathError = path => (path ? `error: ${path}: path must be relative to the project root and must not contain ".."` : 'error: the path argument is missing - give the file to change, e.g. path: "lib/path.h"');

/* Original content (null = file did not exist) of every file written since
   the last resetJournal(), first write wins - what /undo restores. */
const journal = new Map();

export const resetJournal = () => journal.clear();
export const journalPaths = () => [...journal.keys()];

/* number of file writes so far - a loop that wants to know whether *it* changed anything compares before/after */
export let writeCount = 0;

export function undoJournal() {
  const paths = journalPaths();

  for(const [full, original] of journal) {
    if(original === null) remove(full);
    else writeFile(full, original, false);
  }

  journal.clear();
  return paths;
}

function writeFile(full, content, record = true) {
  writeCount++;
  if(record && !journal.has(full)) journal.set(full, fileMode(full) === S_IFREG ? loadFile(full) : null);

  const f = fopen(full, 'w');
  if(!f) return false;
  f.puts(content);
  f.close();
  return true;
}

const shellQuote = s => `'${String(s).replaceAll("'", "'\\''")}'`;

/** "Did you mean" lines for a text that was not found: the lines of `content` containing the longest identifier of `text`. */
function nearLines(content, text) {
  const callee = text.match(/([A-Za-z_]\w*)\s*\(/)?.[1];
  const ids = [...new Set([callee, ...(text.match(/[A-Za-z_]\w{3,}/g) ?? []).sort((a, b) => b.length - a.length)].filter(Boolean))];

  for(const id of ids) {
    const lines = content.split('\n').filter(l => l.includes(id));
    if(lines.length) return `\nLines of the file containing "${id}":\n${lines.slice(0, 4).join('\n')}\nCopy the text to match from these (this tool edits the file given as path, not the file you read the function from).`;
  }

  return '';
}

/** Inserts `text` as new line(s) directly above the (unique) line containing `anchor`. */
function insertAbove({ path, anchor, text }, root) {
  const full = writablePath(path, root);
  if(!full) return pathError(path);
  if(typeof anchor !== 'string' || !anchor || typeof text !== 'string') return 'error: anchor (text of the line to insert above) and text are required';
  if(fileMode(full) !== S_IFREG) {
    console.warn(`warning: file not found or not regular: ${path}`);
    return `error: ${path} not found`;
  }

  const content = loadFile(full);
  let at = content.indexOf(anchor);

  /* Models anchor on the function's *definition* line copied from the .c file
     ("size_t path_rel(char* out, ...) {") when asked to document its
     declaration in the .h. If the exact text is not there but exactly one
     line has "<name>(" for the name the anchor is about, that is the line. */
  if(at === -1) {
    const name = anchor.match(/([A-Za-z_]\w*)\s*\(/)?.[1];
    const re = name && new RegExp(`^[^#\\s/*].*\\b${name}\\s*\\(`, 'gm');
    const hits = re ? [...content.matchAll(re)] : [];
    if(hits.length === 1) at = hits[0].index;
  }

  if(at === -1) return `error: anchor not found in ${path} - it must match a line of that file exactly.${nearLines(content, anchor)}`;
  if(content.indexOf(anchor, at + 1) !== -1 && content.indexOf(anchor) === at) return `error: anchor occurs more than once in ${path} - use more of the line to make it unique`;

  const lineStart = content.lastIndexOf('\n', at - 1) + 1;
  const insert = text.endsWith('\n') ? text : text + '\n';

  if(!writeFile(full, content.slice(0, lineStart) + insert + content.slice(lineStart))) return `error: cannot write ${path}`;

  return `inserted ${insert.split('\n').length - 1} line(s) into ${path} above the line containing the anchor`;
}

/** Exact-text replacement of `oldStr` by `newStr` in one file; the match must be unique. */
function editFile({ path, old_string: oldStr, new_string: newStr }, root) {
  const full = writablePath(path, root);
  if(!full) return pathError(path);
  if(typeof oldStr !== 'string' || !oldStr) return 'error: old_string is required (the exact existing text to replace)';
  if(typeof newStr !== 'string') return 'error: new_string is required';
  if(fileMode(full) !== S_IFREG) {
    console.warn(`warning: file not found or not regular: ${path} - use write_file to create a new file`);
    return `error: ${path} not found - use write_file to create a new file`;
  }

  const content = loadFile(full);
  const first = content.indexOf(oldStr);

  if(first === -1) return `error: old_string not found in ${path} - it must match the file exactly (whitespace included).${nearLines(content, oldStr)}`;

  if(content.indexOf(oldStr, first + 1) !== -1) return `error: old_string occurs more than once in ${path} - include more surrounding lines so it is unique`;

  if(!writeFile(full, content.slice(0, first) + newStr + content.slice(first + oldStr.length))) return `error: cannot write ${path}`;

  const count = s => s.split('\n').length;

  /* Lines of old_string that no longer exist in new_string: a small model
     "inserting" by replacing a span routinely drops neighbouring lines (a
     declaration, a closing brace) without noticing. Say so in the result. */
  const kept = new Set(newStr.split('\n').map(l => l.trim()));
  const dropped = oldStr.split('\n').filter(l => l.trim() && !kept.has(l.trim()));
  const warning = dropped.length
    ? `\nWARNING: this edit REMOVED ${dropped.length} line(s):\n${dropped.slice(0, 6).join('\n')}\nIf that was not intended, put them back now (insert_above).`
    : '';

  return `edited ${path}: ${count(oldStr)} line(s) replaced by ${count(newStr)}${warning}`;
}

/** `{ name, description, parameters }` triples - see API.md's shared
    message/tool-use format; passed straight through to whichever wire
    shape a given client needs (OllamaClient#toTools()/OpenAIClient#toTools()/
    GeminiClient's functionDeclarations wrapping). */
export const TOOLS = [

        path: { type: 'string', description: 'e.g. "lib/path.h"' },

      },
      required: ['path'],
    },
  },
  {
    name: 'grep',

    parameters: {
      type: 'object',
      properties: {
        pattern: { type: 'string', description: 'regular expression' },
        path: { type: 'string', description: 'file or directory to search, default "."' },
      },
      required: ['pattern'],
    },
  },
  {
    name: 'insert_above',
    description: 'Inserts new line(s) directly above an existing line of a file, e.g. a comment above a function declaration. Nothing existing is changed or removed. anchor must identify that line (copy it, or its beginning, from read_file) and occur exactly once in the file.',
    parameters: {
      type: 'object',
      properties: {
        path: { type: 'string', description: 'file to edit, relative to the project root' },
        anchor: { type: 'string', description: 'text of the existing line to insert above (unique in the file)' },
        text: { type: 'string', description: 'the new line(s) to insert' },
      },
      required: ['path', 'anchor', 'text'],
    },
  },
  {
    name: 'edit_file',
    description:
      'Edits an existing file by replacing old_string with new_string. old_string must be the exact existing text (copy it from read_file) and must occur exactly once - include a neighbouring line to make it unique. To only add lines, use insert_above instead. Changes are applied immediately.',
    parameters: {

/**
 * Executes one tool call and returns its result as a string (the `tool`
 * message's `content` - see API.md).
 *
 * @param {string} name
 * @param {object} args
 * @param {string} root
 * @param {(cmd: string) => Promise<boolean>} [confirmRun] - asked before a
 *   `run_command` call actually executes; a declined script is reported
 *   back to the model as declined, not run. Omit to run unconditionally
 *   (used for repl.js's own startup project-scan reads, which never call
 *   `run_command` in the first place).
 * @param {(items: string[], task: string) => Promise<string>} [forEach] -
 *   runs `task` once per item in fresh sub-conversations (repl.js
 *   `runForEach()`); omit inside such a sub-conversation (no nesting).
 * @param {(question: string) => Promise<string>} [askUser] - asked for
 *   `ask_user` calls; omit where there's no user to ask (same startup-scan
 *   case as `confirmRun`).
 */
export async function executeTool(name, args, root, confirmRun, askUser, forEach) {
  /* a tool that throws (e.g. a missing argument) is reported to the model
     like any other tool error, not allowed to abort the whole turn */
  try {
    return await runTool(name, args ?? {}, root, confirmRun, askUser, forEach);
  } catch(e) {
    return `error: ${name} failed: ${e.message} (check the arguments)`;
  }
}

async function runTool(name, args, root, confirmRun, askUser, forEach) {
  if(name === 'list_directory') {
    const dir = args.dir === '.' || !args.dir ? '' : String(args.dir);
    const { base, rel, prefix } = resolveBase(dir, root);
    const files = listFiles(rel, base).map(f => (prefix ? `${prefix}/${f}` : f));
    return files.length ? files.join('\n') : '(no matching files)';
  }

  if(name === 'read_file') {
    if(typeof args.path !== 'string' || !args.path) return 'error: the path argument is missing - give the file to read, e.g. path: "lib/path.h"';
    const { base, rel } = resolveBase(args.path, root);
    const content = readFile(rel, base, args.start, args.end);
    return content;
  }

  if(name === 'grep') {
    if(!args.pattern) return 'error: pattern is required';

    const { output } = await runCommand(`grep -rnE -- ${shellQuote(args.pattern)} ${shellQuote(args.path || '.')} 2>&1 | head -n ${MAX_GREP_LINES}`, { cwd: root });
    return output.trim() ? output : '(no matches)';
  }

  if(name === 'for_each') {
    if(!forEach) return 'error: for_each is not available here';

    let items = args.items;
    if(typeof items === 'string') items = items.trim().startsWith('[') ? JSON.parse(items) : items.split(/[\n,]+/);
    items = (items ?? []).map(i => String(i).trim()).filter(Boolean);

    if(!items.length || typeof args.task !== 'string' || !args.task) return 'error: items (non-empty list) and task are required';

    return await forEach(items, args.task);
  }

  if(name === 'edit_file') return editFile(args, root);

  if(name === 'insert_above') return insertAbove(args, root);

  if(name === 'write_file') {
    const full = writablePath(args.path, root);
    if(!full) return pathError(args.path);
    if(typeof args.content !== 'string') return 'error: content is required';
    if(!writeFile(full, args.content)) return `error: cannot write ${args.path} (does its directory exist?)`;
    return `wrote ${args.path} (${args.content.split('\n').length} lines)`;
  }

  if(name === 'run_command') {
    if(confirmRun && !(await confirmRun(args.command))) return '(declined by user - not executed)';

    const { output, status, timedOut } = await runCommand(args.command, { cwd: root });
    return `(exit ${status}${timedOut ? ', timed out' : ''})\n${output || '(no output)'}`;
  }

  if(name === 'ask_user') {
    if(!askUser) return '(no user prompt mechanism available)';
    return await askUser(args.question);
  }

  /* a foreign tool name (glob, ls, cat, bash, ...) the model used out of habit */
  const alias = aliasToolCall(name, args, TOOLS.map(t => t.name));
  if(alias.name !== name) return runTool(alias.name, alias.args, root, confirmRun, askUser, forEach);

  return `unknown tool: ${name}`;
}

  return a;
}

/**
 * Top-level (first path segment) names under `root` that .gitignore would
 * exclude, via `git ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-standard
 * --directory` - reused so gatherProjectContext()'s layout listing below
 * skips the same build/log/cache noise a git checkout already knows to
 * ignore (CMakeCache.txt, *.log, core dumps, ...) instead of guessing at
 * or hardcoding those patterns itself. Empty Set, not an error, for a
 * root that isn't a git repo at all.
 */
async function gitIgnoredTopLevel(root) {
  const { output, status } = await runCommand('git ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-standard --directory -- .', { cwd: root });
  if(status !== 0) return new Set();

  return new Set(
    output
      .split('\n')
      .filter(Boolean)
      .map(p => (p.indexOf('/') === -1 ? p : p.slice(0, p.indexOf('/')))),
  );
}

/**
 * Runs an automatic list + read of the project root's own layout - a
 * shallow, top-level-only directory listing, plus the root README.md and
 * CMakeLists.txt if the project has them (via executeTool(), the same
 * read_file the model itself calls) - seeded into `messages` once at
 * startup (see main()) so the model always starts a session already
 * knowing the project's shape, the same way Claude Code auto-reads
 * project context rather than waiting to be asked.
 *
 * Deliberately shallow: this used to run a full recursive listing (every
 * listable file in the whole tree, up to 300 entries) and read every
 * README* found anywhere under root, not just the top-level one - on this
 * project alone that came to a ~80KB first-turn payload before a single
 * word had been exchanged, which is what actually triggered
 * BUGS:tls-client-large-body-hangs-or-closes against a real API. A
 * top-level listing plus the project's own README/CMakeLists.txt is
 * enough to know the project's shape; anything deeper is exactly what the
 * tool-calling loop (runToolLoop() above) is for. Files are further
 * filtered to SOURCE_EXT (file-refs.js) - directories always stay (needed
 * to see the layout at all), but a top-level file with no source
 * extension (a cert, a lockfile, a build cache stray SKIP_DIRS/.gitignore
 * didn't already catch) is noise for "what does this project look like",
 * not signal.
 */
async function gatherProjectContext(root) {
  const [names] = readdir(root);
  const ignored = await gitIgnoredTopLevel(root);

  const layout = (names ?? [])
    .filter(name => name !== '.' && name !== '..' && !SKIP_DIRS.has(name) && !ignored.has(name))
    .map(name => ({ name, isDir: fileMode(root === '.' ? name : `${root}/${name}`) === S_IFDIR }))
    .filter(({ name, isDir }) => isDir || SOURCE_EXT.has(name.slice(name.lastIndexOf('.') + 1).toLowerCase()))
    .sort((a, b) => (a.name < b.name ? -1 : a.name > b.name ? 1 : 0))
    .map(({ name, isDir }) => (isDir ? `${name}/` : name))
    .join('\n');

  /* Capped per file: a small model's context is precious, and the full
     text is one read_file away. */
  const reads = [];

  for(const [name, cap] of [['README.md', 3000], ['CLAUDE.md', 3000], ['AGENTS.md', 3000], ['CMakeLists.txt', 1500]]) {
    const text = loadFile(root === '.' ? name : `${root}/${name}`);
    if(text) reads.push(`${name}${text.length > cap ? ` (first ${cap} of ${text.length} chars)` : ''}\n${text.slice(0, cap)}`);
  }

  return `Project layout (top-level only):\n${layout || '(empty)'}${reads.length ? `\n\n${reads.join('\n\n')}` : ''}`;
}

/** one message in full: index, role, char count, then the content and its tool calls, nothing cut */
function messageText(m, i) {
  const content = typeof m.content === 'string' ? m.content : JSON.stringify(m.content ?? '');
  const calls = (m.toolCalls ?? []).map(tc => `[called ${tc.name} ${JSON.stringify(tc.args ?? {})}]`);
  return `[${i}] ${m.role}${m.name ? ` ${m.name}` : ''} (${content.length} chars)\n${content}${calls.length ? `\n${calls.join('\n')}` : ''}`;
}

/** message numbers from "3", "#3", "1-4", "1,3,5" or "all" (whole list `n` long); null when one is not a message */
function messageSelection(text, n) {
  if(/^all$/i.test(text)) return Array.from({ length: n }, (_, i) => i);

  const out = [];

  for(const part of text.split(/[\s,]+/).filter(Boolean)) {
    const m = /^#?(\d+)(?:-#?(\d+))?$/.exec(part);
    if(!m) return null;

    const [a, b] = [+m[1], m[2] === undefined ? +m[1] : +m[2]];
    if(a > b || b >= n) return null;
    for(let i = a; i <= b; i++) if(!out.includes(i)) out.push(i);
  }

  return out.length ? out : null;
}

    .map((m, i) => {

      const preview = content.replaceAll('\n', ' ').slice(0, 80);

const BUILTIN_SYSTEM_PROMPT = `You are a coding assistant that maintains and refactors the code in the
current project directory, like Claude Code. You act through tools: you do
not just describe changes, you make them.

Tools: list_directory, read_file (optionally start/end lines), grep,
insert_above (add lines above an existing line), edit_file (replace exact
existing text), write_file (new files),
for_each (same task over a list of items), run_command (shell; the user
approves it), ask_user.

How to work:
- Look before you change anything: read_file the file you will edit and
  any file whose contents you need to know. Never guess what code does -
  read it. Attached files in the user's message are already read.
- Change files with insert_above (adding lines, e.g. a comment above a
  function) or edit_file (replacing text), in small steps, one change per
  call, with anchor/old_string copied exactly from what you just read.

- A job that repeats over many things is ALWAYS a for_each call, never
  something you do yourself one by one: you only list the items and
  write the task once, for_each does the work. Example - "document every
  function in lib/foo.h from lib/foo/<name>.c": read lib/foo.h (if not
  attached), then call for_each(items=["foo_a", "foo_b", ...], task="In
  lib/foo.h add a 1-2 line comment above the declaration of {item}, based
  on what its source lib/foo/{item}.c does").
- Make only the change that was asked for. Keep the existing code and
  its style as it is: same indentation, same comment style as the
  neighbouring code, no reformatting, no renaming.
- If an edit_file call returns an error, read the file again and retry
  with corrected text.
- When finished, check with run_command (\`git diff\`, build, tests) if
  that is possible, then reply with a short summary of what you changed.
  Do not paste the whole file into your reply.
- Ask the user (ask_user) only if something genuinely cannot be found out
  by looking.

Attached file contents in the user's messages look like:

File: path/to/file.ext
\`\`\`language
...current contents...
\`\`\`
(imports/includes: ../lib/foo.js - read_file any of these you actually need)

The project's top-level layout and README are given below, gathered when
the session started.`;

Tool (read_file): (lines 1-250 of 264)
/**
 * Detects file paths and glob patterns typed into a chat prompt (e.g. "fix
 * the bug in src/foo.js" or "review *.md") and reads their contents so they
 * can be attached to the outgoing message - the same "@file" convenience
 * Claude Code's own prompt does, just token-scanned instead of an explicit
 * `@` syntax.
 */
import { stat, S_IFREG } from 'os';
import { loadFile } from 'std';
import { globMatch, isGlobPattern, walk, fileMode } from './match.js';
import { referenceFiles } from './reference-files.js';
import { directDependencies } from './imports.js';

/* A path-shaped token: contains a `/`, or a glob metacharacter, or ends in
   a plausible file extension. Deliberately permissive - false positives
   are filtered out below by actually trying to resolve them on disk. */
const TOKEN_RE = /[./]*[\w.\-\/*?[\]{}]*[\w*?\]][\w.\-\/*?[\]{}]*/g;

/* what goes into the message as text: a file over ATTACH_MAX_BYTES, generated or binary, or one past the count or total budget
   is only named (see formatFileRefs()) and the model reads it with read_file when it needs it */
const MAX_FILES = 12;
export const ATTACH_MAX_BYTES = 10 * 1024;
const MAX_TOTAL_BYTES = 40 * 1024;
/* largest file read just to count its lines for the reference (and to spot a binary) */
const COUNT_MAX_BYTES = 512 * 1024;
const MAX_REFS = 20;
const BINARY_EXT = /\.(png|jpe?g|gif|webp|ico|bmp|pdf|zip|gz|xz|bz2|zst|tar|7z|so|o|a|dll|exe|bin|wasm|class|jar|mp[34]|mkv|mov|woff2?|ttf|otf|sqlite3?|db|cpio)$/i;
const GENERATED = /(^|\/)(node_modules|\.git|build[^/]*|dist|out)\/|\.min\.(js|css)$|(^|\/)(package-lock\.json|yarn\.lock|pnpm-lock\.yaml)$/;
const MAX_DIR_FILES = 4;
export const SOURCE_EXT = new Set(['js', 'mjs', 'cjs', 'jsx', 'ts', 'tsx', 'c', 'h', 'cpp', 'hpp', 'html', 'css', 'md']);

function looksLikePath(token) {
  if(token.length < 2) return false;
  if(isGlobPattern(token)) {
    // Require at least one alphanumeric character to avoid matching
    // pure glob metacharacters like "***", "**", "???" which commonly
    // appear in make output and would expand to every file.
    return /[A-Za-z0-9]/.test(token);
  }
  if(token.endsWith('/')) return true;
  if(token.includes('/')) return true;
  return /\.[A-Za-z0-9]{1,10}$/.test(token) && !/^\d+\.\d+$/.test(token); // skip bare "2.5"-style numbers
}

/** Up to `MAX_DIR_FILES` source files under `dir` (recursively) - a bare
    directory reference ending in "/" triggers this instead of a single-
    file lookup ("src/" -> a handful of its source files, not a literal
    file named "src/"). Ordered top-level-first (files directly in `dir`
    before anything in a subdirectory), then newest-first within each
    depth - representative of the directory's own top rather than
    whichever subdirectory happens to have the single newest file, and
    capped well below "every file in there" so a broad directory
    reference can't flood the request. */
function newestSourceFiles(dir, root) {
  const base = dir ? (root === '.' ? dir : `${root}/${dir}`) : root;
  const candidates = [];

  for(const path of walk(base, root)) {
    const rel = root === '.' ? path : path.slice(root.length + 1);
    const ext = rel.slice(rel.lastIndexOf('.') + 1).toLowerCase();

    if(!SOURCE_EXT.has(ext)) continue;

    const [st] = stat(root === '.' ? rel : `${root}/${rel}`);
    if(!st) continue;

    const withinDir = dir ? rel.slice(dir.length + 1) : rel;
    const depth = withinDir.split('/').length - 1; // 0 = directly in `dir`, 1 = one subdirectory down, ...

    candidates.push({ path: rel, mtime: st.mtime, depth });
  }

  candidates.sort((a, b) => a.depth - b.depth || b.mtime - a.mtime);

  return candidates.slice(0, MAX_DIR_FILES).map(c => c.path);
}

function stripPunctuation(token) {
  return token.replace(/^[,.;:()'"`]+/, '').replace(/[,.;:()'"`]+$/, '');
}

/**
 * Scans `text` for file/glob references, resolves them against `root`, and
 * returns `{ text, files }` - `files` is `[{ path, content, dependencies }]`
 * for every match that actually resolved to a readable regular file,
 * deduped, capped at `MAX_FILES`/`MAX_TOTAL_BYTES` so a runaway glob can't
 * flood the request. `dependencies` is that file's own direct imports/
 * includes (paths only, see `directDependencies()` - `lib/imports.js`) -
 * not attached, just named, so the model can `read_file` whichever it
 * actually needs instead of every attached file dragging its dependency chain along
 * unasked. `text` is unchanged; callers combine it with `files` themselves
 * (see buildMessage() in repl.js) so detection stays independent of
 * formatting.
 */
export function extractFileRefs(text, root = '.') {
  const candidates = new Set();

  /* "lib/path/<function-name>.c" / "lib/{name}.c" are templates, not paths:
     without this the directory part ("lib/path/") alone was picked up and
     four arbitrary files from it attached. */
  const scanned = text.replace(/\S*[<{][^>}\s]*[>}]\S*/g, ' ');

  for(const raw of scanned.match(TOKEN_RE) ?? []) {
    const token = stripPunctuation(raw);
    if(looksLikePath(token)) candidates.add(token);
  }

  /* label (shown to the model, and used as the write-back path if the
     model echoes it in a "File:" block) -> the actual path to read from -
     the same for project files, but different for referenceFiles() entries
     (label is just the bare name, e.g. "quickjs.h"; the actual path is
     wherever that's installed or checked out). */
  const paths = new Map();

  /* Labels eligible for directDependencies() below - every root-relative
     match (direct file, glob, directory pick), but not a referenceFiles()
     entry: those live outside `root` entirely (an installed/checked-out
     location), so resolving *their* imports/includes against `root`
     wouldn't mean anything. */
  const projectRelative = new Set();
  const referenceFilePaths = referenceFiles(root);

  for(const token of candidates) {
    if(token.endsWith('/')) {
      const dir = token.replace(/\/+$/, '');

      for(const p of newestSourceFiles(dir, root)) {
        paths.set(p, root === '.' ? p : `${root}/${p}`);
        projectRelative.add(p);
      }
      continue;
    }

    if(isGlobPattern(token)) {
      for(const p of globMatch(token, root)) {
        paths.set(p, root === '.' ? p : `${root}/${p}`);
        projectRelative.add(p);
      }
      continue;
    }

    const full = root === '.' ? token : `${root}/${token}`;

    if(fileMode(full) === S_IFREG) {
      paths.set(token, full);
      projectRelative.add(token);
    } else if(referenceFilePaths[token]) {
      paths.set(token, referenceFilePaths[token]);
    }
  }

  const files = [];
  let totalBytes = 0;
  const skipped = [];
  const large = [];

  for(const [label, full] of paths) {
    const [st] = stat(full);

    if(!st || BINARY_EXT.test(label)) {
      skipped.push(label);
      continue;
    }

    const why = GENERATED.test(label) ? 'generated' : st.size > ATTACH_MAX_BYTES ? 'too large' : files.length >= MAX_FILES || totalBytes + st.size > MAX_TOTAL_BYTES ? 'over the budget' : null;

    if(why) {
      /* named only: its size and lines tell the model whether to read it whole or by line range */
      const text = st.size <= COUNT_MAX_BYTES ? loadFile(full) : null;
      if(text?.includes('\0')) skipped.push(label);
      else large.push({ path: label, size: st.size, lines: text == null ? null : text.split('\n').length - (text.endsWith('\n') ? 1 : 0), why });
      continue;
    }

    const content = loadFile(full);
    if(content == null || content.includes('\0')) {
      skipped.push(label);
      continue;
    }

    const dependencies = projectRelative.has(label) ? directDependencies(label, content, root) : [];

    files.push({ path: label, content, dependencies });
    totalBytes += st.size;
  }

  return { files, skipped, large };
}

const EXT_LANG = {
  js: 'javascript',
  mjs: 'javascript',
  cjs: 'javascript',
  ts: 'typescript',
  jsx: 'jsx',
  tsx: 'tsx',
  json: 'json',
  py: 'python',
  c: 'c',
  h: 'c',
  cpp: 'cpp',
  hpp: 'cpp',
  sh: 'bash',
  md: 'markdown',
  html: 'html',
  css: 'css',
  yml: 'yaml',
  yaml: 'yaml',
  log: 'text',
  txt: 'cmake',
  cmake: 'cmake',
  ac: 'aclocal',
  in: 'autotools',
  am: 'automake',
  'clang-format': 'yaml',
  crt: 'pem/x509',
  key: 'pem/x509',
  pem: 'pem',
  'sublime-project': 'json/sublime-text',
  'sublime-workspace': 'json/sublime-text',
  prettierrc: 'json',
  git: 'text',
  gitignore: 'magic',
  'cmake-format': 'magic',
  gitmodules: 'text',
  diff: 'diff',
  patch: 'diff',
  sqlite: 'sqlite',
  sqlite3: 'sqlite',
  project: 'xml/eclipse',
  cproject: 'xml/eclipse',
};

export function languageFor(path) {
  const ext = path.slice(path.lastIndexOf('.') + 1).toLowerCase();
  return EXT_LANG[ext] ?? '';
}

/** Renders `files` as "File: path" + fenced-code blocks (plus a
    dependencies note when `extractFileRefs()` found any - see its own
    doc comment), the same shape the assistant is instructed (see
    SYSTEM_PROMPT in repl.js) to reply with. */
/** "53 KB", "812 B", "1.4 MB" */
const fmtSize = n => (n < 1024 ? `${n} B` : n < 1048576 ? `${Math.round(n / 102.4) / 10} KB` : `${Math.round(n / 104857.6) / 10} MB`);

/** the files that were not attached, by name in backticks with size and lines, and how to get at them */
export function formatFileRefs(large) {
  if(!large?.length) return '';

  const rows = large.slice(0, MAX_REFS).map(f => `- \`${f.path}\` (${f.lines != null ? `${f.lines} lines, ` : ''}${fmtSize(f.size)}${f.why === 'too large' ? '' : `, ${f.why}`})`);

Assistant:

Tool (read_file): (lines 250-264 of 264)
  const rows = large.slice(0, MAX_REFS).map(f => `- \`${f.path}\` (${f.lines != null ? `${f.lines} lines, ` : ''}${fmtSize(f.size)}${f.why === 'too large' ? '' : `, ${f.why}`})`);
  if(large.length > MAX_REFS) rows.push(`- ... and ${large.length - MAX_REFS} more`);

  return `Not attached (too large, generated or over the size budget of this message): read the part you need with read_file (path, and start/end for a line range), do not guess what is in them:\n${rows.join('\n')}`;
}

export function formatFileBlocks(files) {
  return files
    .map(f => {
      const deps = f.dependencies?.length ? `\n(imports/includes: ${f.dependencies.join(', ')} - read_file any of these you actually need)` : '';
      return `File: ${f.path}\n\`\`\`${languageFor(f.path)}\n${f.content}\n\`\`\`${deps}`;
    })
    .join('\n\n');
}

/** Path inside `root` that a tool may write: relative, no "..". Returns the full path or null. */
function writablePath(path, root) {
  if(typeof path !== 'string' || !path) {
    console.warn('warning: path argument is missing or not a string');
    return null;
  }
  if( path.startsWith('/') || path.split('/').includes('..')) {
    console.warn('warning: path starts with / or contains .. segment and is rejected');
    return null;
  }
  return root === '.' ? path : `${root}/${path}`;
}

const pathError = path => (path ? `error: ${path}: path must be relative to the project root and must not contain ".."` : 'error: the path argument is missing - give the file to change, e.g. path: "lib/path.h"');

/* Original content (null = file did not exist) of every file written since
   the last resetJournal(), first write wins - what /undo restores. */
const journal = new Map();

export const resetJournal = () => journal.clear();
export const journalPaths = () => [...journal.keys()];

/* number of file writes so far - a loop that wants to know whether *it* changed anything compares before/after */
export let writeCount = 0;

export function undoJournal() {
  const paths = journalPaths();

  for(const [full, original] of journal) {
    if(original === null) remove(full);
    else writeFile(full, original, false);
  }

  journal.clear();
  return paths;
}

function writeFile(full, content, record = true) {
  writeCount++;
  if(record && !journal.has(full)) journal.set(full, fileMode(full) === S_IFREG ? loadFile(full) : null);

  const f = fopen(full, 'w');
  if(!f) return false;
  f.puts(content);
  f.close();
  return true;
}

const shellQuote = s => `'${String(s).replaceAll("'", "'\\''")}'`;

/** "Did you mean" lines for a text that was not found: the lines of `content` containing the longest identifier of `text`. */
function nearLines(content, text) {
  const callee = text.match(/([A-Za-z_]\w*)\s*\(/)?.[1];
  const ids = [...new Set([callee, ...(text.match(/[A-Za-z_]\w{3,}/g) ?? []).sort((a, b) => b.length - a.length)].filter(Boolean))];

  for(const id of ids) {
    const lines = content.split('\n').filter(l => l.includes(id));
    if(lines.length) return `\nLines of the file containing "${id}":\n${lines.slice(0, 4).join('\n')}\nCopy the text to match from these (this tool edits the file given as path, not the file you read the function from).`;
  }

  return '';
}

/** Inserts `text` as new line(s) directly above the (unique) line containing `anchor`. */
function insertAbove({ path, anchor, text }, root) {
  const full = writablePath(path, root);
  if(!full) return pathError(path);
  if(typeof anchor !== 'string' || !anchor || typeof text !== 'string') return 'error: anchor (text of the line to insert above) and text are required';
  if(fileMode(full) !== S_IFREG) {
    console.warn(`warning: file not found or not regular: ${path}`);
    return `error: ${path} not found`;
  }

  const content = loadFile(full);
  let at = content.indexOf(anchor);

  /* Models anchor on the function's *definition* line copied from the .c file
     ("size_t path_rel(char* out, ...) {") when asked to document its
     declaration in the .h. If the exact text is not there but exactly one
     line has "<name>(" for the name the anchor is about, that is the line. */
  if(at === -1) {
    const name = anchor.match(/([A-Za-z_]\w*)\s*\(/)?.[1];
    const re = name && new RegExp(`^[^#\\s/*].*\\b${name}\\s*\\(`, 'gm');
    const hits = re ? [...content.matchAll(re)] : [];
    if(hits.length === 1) at = hits[0].index;
  }

  if(at === -1) return `error: anchor not found in ${path} - it must match a line of that file exactly.${nearLines(content, anchor)}`;
  if(content.indexOf(anchor, at + 1) !== -1 && content.indexOf(anchor) === at) return `error: anchor occurs more than once in ${path} - use more of the line to make it unique`;

  const lineStart = content.lastIndexOf('\n', at - 1) + 1;

## Step 1: Update System Prompt in `repl.js`

Tool (read_file): (lines 331-345 of 5647)
const BUILTIN_SYSTEM_PROMPT = `You are a coding assistant that maintains and refactors the code in the
current project directory, like Claude Code. You act through tools: you do
not just describe changes, you make them.

Tools: list_directory, read_file (optionally start/end lines), grep,
insert_above (add lines above an existing line), edit_file (replace exact
existing text), write_file (new files),
for_each (same task over a list of items), run_command (shell; the user
approves it), ask_user.

How to work:
- Look before you change anything: read_file the file you will edit and

const BUILTIN_SYSTEM_PROMPT = `You are a coding assistant that maintains and refactors the code in the
current project directory, like Claude Code. You act through tools: you do
not just describe changes, you make them.

Tools: list_directory, read_file (optionally start/end lines), grep,
insert_above (add lines above an existing line), edit_file (replace exact
existing text), write_file (new files),
for_each (same task over a list of items), run_command (shell; the user
approves it), ask_user.

How to work:
- Look before you change anything: read_file the file you will edit and
  any file whose contents you need to know. Never guess what code does -
  read it. Attached files in the user's message are already read.
- Change files with insert_above (adding lines, e.g. a comment above a
  function) or edit_file (replacing text), in small steps, one change per
  call, with anchor/old_string copied exactly from what you just read.

- A job that repeats over many things is ALWAYS a for_each call, never
  something you do yourself one by one: you only list the items and
  write the task once, for_each does the work. Example - "document every
  function in lib/foo.h from lib/foo/<name>.c": read lib/foo.h (if not
  attached), then call for_each(items=["foo_a", "foo_b", ...], task="In
  lib/foo.h add a 1-2 line comment above the declaration of {item}, based
  on what its source lib/foo/{item}.c does").
- Make only the change that was asked for. Keep the existing code and
  its style as it is: same indentation, same comment style as the
  neighbouring code, no reformatting, no renaming.
- If an edit_file call returns an error, read the file again and retry
  with corrected text.
- When finished, check with run_command (\`git diff\`, build, tests) if
  that is possible, then reply with a short summary of what you changed.
  Do not paste the whole file into your reply.
- Ask the user (ask_user) only if something genuinely cannot be found out
  by looking.

const BUILTIN_SYSTEM_PROMPT = `You are a coding assistant that maintains and refactors the code in the
current project directory, like Claude Code. You act through tools: you do
not just describe changes, you make them.

Path Conventions:
~ refers to your home directory (e.g., ~/config.json).
./ refers to the project root (current working directory). The project root is currently: [INSERT_ROOT_HERE].
Absolute paths (e.g., /home/user/project/src/foo.js) are allowed but will be shown to you as relative paths (e.g., ./src/foo.js) if they fall within the project root.
When calling tools (read_file, edit_file, etc.), prefer ./ relative paths. You may use ~ for homedir files.
Never use .. to escape the project root.

*Implementation note:* Since `opts.root` can change via `/cd`, the root is injected dynamically when the prompt is built, but the above rules are always in effect.

Tools: list_directory, read_file (optionally start/end lines), grep,
insert_above (add lines above an existing line), edit_file (replace exact
existing text), write_file (new files),
for_each (same task over a list of items), run_command (shell; the user
approves it), ask_user.

  any file whose contents you need to know. Never guess what code does -

  function) or edit_file (replacing text), in small steps, one change per

  something you do yourself one by one: you only list the items and
  write the task once, for_each does the work. Example - "document every
  function in lib/foo.h from lib/foo/<name>.c": read lib/foo.h (if not
  attached), then call for_each(items=["foo_a", "foo_b", ...], task="In
  lib/foo.h add a 1-2 line comment above the declaration of {item}, based
  on what its source lib/foo/{item}.c does").
- Make only the change that was asked for. Keep the existing code and
  its style as it is: same indentation, same comment style as the
  neighbouring code, no reformatting, no renaming.
- If an edit_file call returns an error, read the file again and retry

Tool (read_file): (lines 3925-3955 of 5659)

  /* Runs `task` once per item, each in its own fresh two-message
     conversation (system prompt + the task for that one item) - a small
     model is far more reliable on "do this one thing" than on "do this to
     all 20 things" in one ever-growing context. Backs both the /each
     command and the model's own for_each tool. */
  const forEach = async (items, task) => {
    /* in the TUI the items show their tool lines, panes and replies like any turn (quiet hid all of it, verbose mode included); piped they stay short */
    const sub = { ...opts, stream: false, quiet: !tui?.active, maxRounds: Math.min(opts.maxRounds, 12), omitTools: omitToolsFor(task) };
    const hasSlot = /\{item\}|\{\}/.test(task);
    const results = [];

    for(const [i, item] of items.entries()) {

Assistant: Now let's move to Step 2 - Outbound Path Normalization. First, let me add the `toRelativePath` helper to `lib/paths.js`:


  [called read_file {"path":"lib/paths.js"}]

Tool (read_file): /**
 * paths: shell-style home expansion for paths the user types (/cd, Tab
 * completion).
 *
 *   expandHome('~/Sources')        -> '/home/roman/Sources'
 *   expandHome('~rsenn/x')         -> '/home/rsenn/x'   (from /etc/passwd)
 *   expandHome('src')              -> 'src'
 *   unquote('"My Dir"'), unquote('My\\ Dir')  -> 'My Dir'
 */
import { getenv, loadFile } from 'std';

/** home directory of `user` from /etc/passwd, undefined if unknown */
export function homeOf(user) {
  for(const line of (loadFile('/etc/passwd') ?? '').split('\n')) {
    const f = line.split(':');
    if(f[0] === user && f[5]) return f[5];
  }
}

/** path with a leading ~ or ~user replaced by that home directory, else unchanged */
export function expandHome(path) {
  const m = /^~([^/]*)(\/.*)?$/.exec(path);
  if(!m) return path;

  const home = m[1] ? homeOf(m[1]) : (getenv('HOME') ?? homeOf(getenv('USER') ?? ''));
  return home ? home.replace(/\/$/, '') + (m[2] ?? '') : path;
}

/** surrounding quotes and backslash escapes removed */
export function unquote(s) {
  s = s.trim();
  if(s.length > 1 && (s[0] === '"' || s[0] === "'") && s[s.length - 1] === s[0]) return s.slice(1, -1);
  return s.replace(/\\(.)/g, '$1');
}

You: ## PROJECT CONTEXT
You are working on `ollama-repl`, a Claude-Code-style terminal agent REPL written in JavaScript for the QuickJS (`qjsm`) runtime. 
- Runtime: QuickJS only. No Node.js. No `require`, no `node:path`, no `node:fs`. Use `std`, `os`, and `lib/paths.js` helpers.
- Main file: `repl.js` (~2700 lines). Holds the main closure, system prompts, and command routing.
- Key path files: `lib/paths.js` (expandHome, unquote), `lib/file-refs.js` (attachFiles, formatFileBlocks), `lib/file-blocks.js` (isSafeRelativePath), `lib/tool-requests.js` (executeTool).
- The project root/current working directory is held in `opts.root` (changeable via `/cd`).

## THE TASK
Update the harness so the model understands and uses homedir (`~`), relative (`./`), and absolute paths correctly. This requires changes to the system prompt, outbound path normalization (harness -> model), and inbound path resolution (model -> harness).

### 1. Update the System Prompt
In `repl.js`, update `BUILTIN_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (and ensure it flows through to `SYSTEM_PROMPT`). Add a clear "Path Conventions" section. 

Add this text to the prompt:
Path Conventions:
~ refers to your home directory (e.g., ~/config.json).
./ refers to the project root (current working directory). The project root is currently: [INSERT_ROOT_HERE].
Absolute paths (e.g., /home/user/project/src/foo.js) are allowed but will be shown to you as relative paths (e.g., ./src/foo.js) if they fall within the project root.
When calling tools (read_file, edit_file, etc.), prefer ./ relative paths. You may use ~ for homedir files.
Never use .. to escape the project root.
*Implementation detail:* Since `opts.root` can change via `/cd`, you should either inject the current root dynamically when the prompt is built, or just state the rule and let the tool results show the relative paths. If injecting dynamically, update `messages[0].content` when `/cd` is run (similar to how `systemNotes(opts.root)` is already appended).

### 2. Outbound Path Normalization (Harness -> Model)
Before sending text to the model (in prompts, attached files, or tool results), absolute paths that reside *inside* `opts.root` must be rewritten as `./relative/path`.

Places to update:
- `lib/file-refs.js`: In `formatFileBlocks()` and `formatFileRefs()`, rewrite the `path` property of attached files to be relative to `opts.root` (prefixed with `./`) if they are inside it.
- `repl.js`: In `gatherProjectContext()`, rewrite the layout listing paths to use `./`.
- `lib/tool-requests.js`: In the output of `read_file`, `list_directory`, and `grep`, rewrite any absolute file paths in the output text to be relative to `opts.root` (prefixed with `./`).
- `repl.js`: In `formatFileBlocks` or wherever `File: path` headers are generated for the model's own writes, ensure they are relative.

*Helper logic:* Create or use a helper function `toRelativePath(absPath, root)`:
```javascript
// Returns './relative/path' if absPath is inside root, else returns absPath unchanged.
function toRelativePath(absPath, root) {
  const normRoot = root.endsWith('/') ? root : root + '/';
  if (absPath.startsWith(normRoot)) {
    return './' + absPath.slice(normRoot.length);
  }
  if (absPath === root) return './';
  return absPath;
}
3. Inbound Path Resolution (Model -> Harness)
When the model calls a tool with a path, the harness must correctly resolve it before checking safety or reading the file.
lib/paths.js: Ensure expandHome(path) is called on all inbound tool arguments. It already handles ~ and ~user.
lib/file-blocks.js / lib/tool-requests.js: Update isSafeRelativePath (or the path validation logic in executeTool) to:
Accept paths starting with ./.
Accept paths starting with ~ (after expanding them).
Accept absolute paths, BUT resolve them and verify they fall within opts.root. If an absolute path is inside opts.root, normalize it to a relative path internally before processing.
Reject paths containing .. that would escape opts.root.
4. Constraints & Rules
Do NOT use Node's path module. Use string manipulation or existing lib/paths.js functions.
opts.root is the source of truth for the "current directory". It is an absolute path (resolved via realpath in resolveDir).
When rewriting paths in tool results, be careful not to break JSON formatting or code blocks. Only rewrite paths that are clearly file paths (e.g., in read_file headers, list_directory outputs, or File: headers).
Run cmake . and cmake --install . in the repo root after editing files under lib/.
Run smoke.sh to verify the harness still loads and basic commands work.
5. Verification Steps
Start a session. Check the system prompt (/system) to ensure the Path Conventions section is present and mentions the current root.
Ask the model to list_directory the root. Verify the output shows ./filename instead of /absolute/filename.

Check that edit_file with a ./ path works.
Check that edit_file with ../ (escaping root) is rejected.

/**
 * paths: shell-style home expansion for paths the user types (/cd, Tab
 * completion).
 *
 *   expandHome('~/Sources')        -> '/home/roman/Sources'
 *   expandHome('~rsenn/x')         -> '/home/rsenn/x'   (from /etc/passwd)
 *   expandHome('src')              -> 'src'
 *   unquote('"My Dir"'), unquote('My\\ Dir')  -> 'My Dir'
 */
import { getenv, loadFile } from 'std';

/** home directory of `user` from /etc/passwd, undefined if unknown */
export function homeOf(user) {
  for(const line of (loadFile('/etc/passwd') ?? '').split('\n')) {
    const f = line.split(':');
    if(f[0] === user && f[5]) return f[5];
  }
}

/** path with a leading ~ or ~user replaced by that home directory, else unchanged */
export function expandHome(path) {
  const m = /^~([^/]*)(\/.*)?$/.exec(path);
  if(!m) return path;

  const home = m[1] ? homeOf(m[1]) : (getenv('HOME') ?? homeOf(getenv('USER') ?? ''));
  return home ? home.replace(/\/$/, '') + (m[2] ?? '') : path;
}

/** surrounding quotes and backslash escapes removed */
export function unquote(s) {
  s = s.trim();
  if(s.length > 1 && (s[0] === '"' || s[0] === "'") && s[s.length - 1] === s[0]) return s.slice(1, -1);
